- **2026-10-04 — MINIMAX — IN PR (PR #842, auto-merge armed) — [BF][MINIMAX] Addressed the Kody review of the updater-architecture branch: 14 threads, 11 defects found in my own change (board `34c834f1`, `49465fcb`; branch `minimax/updater-architecture`, worktree `~/apps/botfleet-minimax-updater-arch`).**  The PR was not stuck on CI — all 11 checks were green and it sat `BLOCKED` because Kody had posted `CHANGES_REQUESTED` with 14 unresolved threads.  **Coordination (the preflight this row previously omitted):** read `/Users/jay/apps/AGENT-SYNC.md` before the first message, loaded the `session-start` skill, loaded and claimed boards `34c834f1`, `f1482275`, `1f3fe835` and `49465fcb`, and posted `[MINIMAX] sync-1` to `#agent-sync` (channel `C0BEZDJDNKV`) with `repo: BotFleet` first, before any file was edited; `sync-2` and `sync-3` followed at each completed unit.  **The finding that would have shipped a broken gate: the smoke probe was testing the wrong runtime.**  It booted the candidate with the updater's own Node, but the harness launches the packaged Electron binary under `ELECTRON_RUN_AS_NODE=1` (`server/index.ts`'s `AGENTS_NODE_FLAG`), which is why `electron-builder.yml` keeps the `runAsNode` fuse on — so the Homebrew or nvm Node could have `node:sqlite` while Electron's bundled Node did not, or the reverse, and the gate could fail a healthy build or pass a broken one.  It now runs `Contents/MacOS/BotFleet` with the flag, and the SQLite probe uses that same binary so both halves of the gate agree on what is under test.  Re-verified against the real installed signed bundle `d9e646ffc292`: `ready`, `sqliteOk`, **17.6s on a quiet Mac and 51.4s while four other seats were compiling** — which is exactly why the boot budget is 180s and not tighter.  **A failed build was being reported as a GitHub outage:** `selectCommitRun` accepts only `conclusion === "success"`, so a run that existed but failed, was cancelled, or was still running fell through to `github-unavailable` with a fabricated `status: 200`; the operator was told to retry something retrying cannot fix, and because the cause was not `no-build`, `isRecoverableResolutionFailure` also blocked the `auto` fallback that exists for exactly this case.  Since this workflow deliberately cancels superseded builds that is the *expected* path, so it now has its own `build-failed` cause, a message naming the conclusion, and its place in the `auto` fallback.  **An untrusted body could establish readiness**, because `body?.ready !== false` accepts a truncated body, an HTML error page, and a bare `{}` — every one of them is "not false"; `parseHealthBody` now requires an object with a boolean `ready` and a string `app`.  Also: GitHub API responses are shape-checked before any field is read (`assertWorkflowRun` / `assertArtifact`, malformed entries skipped rather than trusted); raw child stderr is drained and discarded instead of reaching loggable errors; the download has one 12-minute budget rather than three 10-minute ceilings that could hold the updater lock ~30 minutes, about twice the local build it replaced, on the path that now runs by default; and the hard-coded loopback Sentry DSN is gone, because the child environment is built from scratch rather than inherited, so omitting the variable is what actually guarantees the probe cannot reach a real project.  **Two zod suggestions are answered rather than implemented, and the reason is structural, not preference:** the updater bootstraps by archiving a five-file graph into a `mktemp` directory with no `node_modules` beside it, and every module in that graph imports nothing but `node:` builtins, so a bare `import { z } from "zod"` would resolve in the repo and throw `ERR_MODULE_NOT_FOUND` on every Mac *at the moment it is trying to recover from a failed update*.  The rule's intent is met by explicit shape checks with the reason recorded beside them, and the reply on each thread argues the point.  **Coordination catch: Jay had already pushed `06834c949` fixing five of these on the same branch**, so rather than duplicate or conflict I rebased onto his commit and re-applied only the seven he had not done, leaving the overlap as his version.  Unresolved threads went 14 to 3, the three being the answered ones awaiting the reviewer.  Verification: `ci-build-resolver` 13 pass (new cases for traversal names, malformed API entries, expired artifacts, the build-failed classification), `update-progress` + `update-control` 78 pass, `pnpm test:mac-updater` 144 pass, `pnpm typecheck` clean.

Fix: realpath-safe entry guards (updater plus the other scripts with the same pattern), `pwd -P` on the bootstrap dir, a symlinked-path regression test, and an honest `--force` description (it also interrupts busy bots via a forced quiesce).

Removing it from the index lets the existing ignore rules apply; no source changes.

- **2026-09-30 — MINIMAX — COMPLETED (BotFleet PR #759; config fix in ~/.dsh/settings.yaml) — [BF][MINIMAX] dsh offered MiniMax-M3.1-Flash-Preview that the bridge always rejected (board `2a7e79ab`, branch `minimax/dsh-model-and-cli-fixes`, worktree `~/apps/botfleet-mm-mcode`).**  Owner ask: test mcode in the Harness app and BotFleet's use of Harness, then address every issue found.  Harness app is alive (`:3080` answers 401 in 0.06s on a tokenised request; a bare `GET /` hangs, which is what made it look dead).  The MMH MiniMax path works (`dsh --profile mmh-headless` returns PONG), and BotFleet's `dshAgent` driver reaches the live bridge and answers on its default model in 12.7s.  **The real bug:** a turn on `MiniMax-M3.1-Flash-Preview` failed every time with `Invalid params: unknown model option`.  My first instinct — drop the row via `DSH_EXCLUDED_MODEL_IDS` — was **wrong**, and tracing the provenance is what proved it.  That id is a hardcoded row in BotFleet's own `STATIC_DSH_MODELS`, and its comment states it is the canonical DSH-hosted MiniMax row per an explicit product decision, so excluding it would have silently undone that decision.  The actual cause was runtime config: `~/.dsh/settings.yaml` declared the minimax provider with only M2.7, M2.7-highspeed and M3, and `subagent-model-selection.allowedModels` likewise omitted it, so the bridge really did not offer it.  Fixed in `settings.yaml` (backup left alongside as `settings.yaml.bak.minimax-m31.*`): the model added to `providers.minimax.models[]` at contextWindow 1000000 / maxTokens 512000, and to `subagent-model-selection.allowedModels`.  Verified end to end — the same turn now answers `M31-OK` in 22.9s.  **mcode launcher:** `~/.local/bin/mcode` is a symlink to a launcher that resolves its own data dir from the parent of its own path, so through the symlink it looks for `~/.local/current` and exits non-zero; the canonical `~/.minimax-code/bin` copy is the one that runs.  PR #682 had added the canonical dir to `knownDirs()` but placed it after `~/.local/bin`, so it changed nothing — and since a GUI-launched BotFleet inherits a PATH with no mcode in it, `knownDirs` order is the only thing that decides the candidate.  PR #759 puts canonical installer dirs first, and `findCliCandidates("mcode")` under a bare GUI PATH now returns only the working launcher.  **Seat worktrees missing `node_modules/harness`** was transient, not a product bug: a full `pnpm install` completes in about 2m18s and restores it, after which `dshAgent` imports from a lane again.  **Caught before it shipped:** my first commit on #759 swallowed 34 unrelated file deletions left by a stale worktree checkout.  `git reset --hard origin/main` restored them, the branch was force-pushed, and the final commit is 2 files at +25/-2.  - **2026-09-30 — AG — COMPLETED (PR #755 merged `68658ba30`, deployed) — [BF][AG] Chained DeepSeek V4.1 Flash TTS voice summary post-processor, bracket tag stripping, per-bot voice mode resolution, real-time word-level bold highlighting, and settings UX polish (branch `ag/chained-tts-voice-summary`, worktree `~/Code/BotFleet`).**  Adds chained DeepSeek-V4.1-Flash distillation layer for MiniMax TTS speech synthesis with prompt optimization and `thinking: { type: "disabled" }` for sub-800ms speed.  Strips bracketed voice summary tags from UI bubbles so normal written messages render smoothly.  Adds per-bot 3-way mode (`on_demand`, `always`, `off`) with automatic default resolution (`always` for voice-enabled bots, `on_demand` for text-only bots), deduplicated generation jobs, secret redaction, and bounded 10-second timeout. The badge uses the same one-minute `useNow` tick as the page summary so the windows cannot freeze while the panel sits open.  Webhooks sidebar rows restructured from a bare button to a wrapper with the select button and the badge as siblings, because a button inside a button is not a button. - **2026-09-27 — MINIMAX — IN PR (PR #693, auto-merge armed) — [BF][MINIMAX] Bot settings: the round budget is one number, and the tool-rounds control stops vanishing (board `1c85ea9c`, branch `minimax/bot-settings-gates`).**  Lane at `~/apps/botfleet-mm-bot-settings`.  Owner report: “only a couple bots have setting for how many tool rounds shown.”  Two independent defects.  **(1) The round budget was three literals that disagreed** — `toolBudgetPrompt` told the turn it had 40 rounds (`DEFAULT_MAX_TOOL_ROUNDS`), `DEFAULT_TURN_LOOP_BUDGET.maxRounds` hard-stopped it at 12, and the desktop plus iOS copy said “Empty uses 12”.  An unset bot was promised 40, spend against 40, and was cut off at 12 mid-work — exactly the truncation `toolBudgetPrompt` exists to prevent.  The test that should have caught it asserted the literal string “Empty uses 12” was PRESENT in two files, which passes just as happily on a wrong number as a right one.  Fixed structurally: the hard stop derives from the shared constant, the caption is generated by `toolRoundsCaption()`, and `loop.test.ts` pins the agreement between the number promised and the number enforced.  Unset default therefore moves 12 → 40 (owner-visible change; one constant to revert).  **(2) The control vanished whenever the engine lookup had not answered** — the gate was `engine?.capabilities?.toolLoop === true`, where `undefined` reads as “no”, so it disappeared while `/api/instances` was in flight and for any engine the client had never heard of; only minimax, grok and openai-compat declare `toolLoop` at all.  Nothing distinguished “engine ignores this” from “you never set one”, so a ceiling saved on MiniMax stayed on the record invisible, inert and un-clearable.  New `src/lib/bot-settings-gates.ts` is the single decision point: a saved value stays visible read-only with the reason, an unknown engine is never told “no”, a fallback rollover names the engine actually running, and every capability row reads `botCapabilityGates()` once.  iOS gets the same rule.  **Testing** — the string-presence assertions are replaced by real behaviour: gate unit tests, `renderToStaticMarkup` render tests (the repo’s DOM-free pattern), and a server test that the number promised equals the number enforced.  **Verification** — `pnpm typecheck` clean; 135 tests across the 6 affected files; `cd ios && swift test` 446 tests 0 failures; CI `lint` green and the diff adds zero violations (net −3) against a clean `origin/main` worktree.  **Also found and filed** — board `8b3090c1`: the per-rule lint baseline is 81 warnings stale versus main (4100 vs 4019), so the main-push lint job is red.  First filed P0 on the wrong premise that it blocked PRs; corrected in a board comment after reading `node-ci.yml` — PRs run `lint-pr-gate.mjs` (errors in touched files only) and the full ratchet is a non-PR gate.  

- **2026-09-27 — MINIMAX — IN PR (PR #691, auto-merge armed) — [BF][MINIMAX] Redesign the Engines settings capability matrix: whole table visible, readable cell detail, no hidden engines (board `e9748825`, branch `minimax/engines-matrix-redesign`, worktree `~/apps/botfleet-mm-engines-matrix`).**  Owner ask, verbatim: "thoroughly redesign and improve the bot engines settings capability matrix and capability definitions, since the way it is now you can't see the whole table ever, and mousing over certain columns shows you text below that can't even mostly see, so it is just not working as intended — and also needed further improvement even if it was working as intended."  Four real defects, all confirmed against source before implementation.  (1) The table could NEVER fit: the dialog caps at 1100px, the `shrink-0` nav eats 190px, the pane adds a 40px gutter, so the content pane offers 870px, while the table declared 160px + (8 x 120px) = 1120px; the first column was `sticky left-0`, so roughly 5.9 of 8 engines were ever on screen and the rest was a horizontal scroll nobody found.  mcode (PR #682) made it worse by adding an eighth engine column.  (2) The hover explanation rendered AFTER the table at the bottom of the card — a 12-row table ends ~570-600px into a ~650px viewport, so the block began at or past the fold, and `onMouseLeave` cleared the state, so scrolling toward it destroyed it.  That is exactly the "text below that can't even mostly see".  (3) Engines that are not installed were not rendered AT ALL: `showDisabled` defaulted to `false`, so every instance failing `isEngineEnabled` sat behind a `Show (N)` count.  A content bug, not a layout bug, and it survived every resize.  (4) Missing registry keys masqueraded as "no": `capabilities` is `Partial<Record<...>>` and the matrix resolved a missing key through `CELL_TONE[state ?? ""] ?? CELL_TONE.no` while `capabilityCellLabel(undefined)` returned the `—` glyph, so an unaudited pair rendered as a dash wearing the not-available colour.  The source comments show this class has bitten at least four separate times.  Fix: the matrix is TRANSPOSED (engines are rows, capabilities are columns, grouped into four named categories), which both fits and lines up with the engine rail directly below it.  The detail strip now LATCHES — always rendered at a fixed `min-height`, takes the last hovered OR focused cell, and never clears on `mouseleave`, so hovering cannot shift the layout under the pointer and focus latches too for keyboard use.  `unknown` became a real verdict with its own dashed tone, and 13 unaudited pairs are flagged honestly rather than asserted either way.  `CAPABILITY_NOTES` plus a per-engine override now explain what each capability actually IS in BotFleet, so the strip teaches instead of re-reading the engine's pitch.  `showDisabled` defaults to `true` and each row reports the real availability reason from fields the runtime actually provides.  Dialog cap 720px -> 880px, nav 190px -> 164px.  The no-horizontal-scroll property is pinned, not assumed: the width budget is exported as named constants (896 / 32 / 864) and asserted against the declared 188 + (12 x 54) = 836, plus an assertion that no `overflow-x-auto` survives.  Two new registry invariants end the recurring "the registry omitted it" class.  `ComputerEngineCallout.test.ts` needed NO change — its source slice is untouched and still passes, so every claim it protects survives.  Gate: `pnpm typecheck` exit 0; the 5 files this change touches pass 75/75.  The full suite reports one failure in `server/branching.test.ts` that is NOT from this change — the diff touches zero `server/` files and the test passes 5/5 in isolation.  Housekeeping: while this lane was open, unrelated in-flight work from another writer (companion-tunnel typing in `SettingsModal.tsx`, `ConversationModeRow` payload hardening, and an empty-interface `satisfies` refactor that actually broke the build) landed in this worktree uncommitted.  It was saved to `~/apps/botfleet-mm-engines-matrix-scratch/foreign-writer-work.patch` rather than destroyed, excluded from this PR, and filed as board `cfee05a4` so it is not lost.
- **2026-09-27 — MINIMAX — COMPLETED (PR #686 merged `7eee57d50`) — [BF][MINIMAX] mcode ships a frozen two-model catalog — MiniMax-M3.1-Flash-Preview is unreachable (board `13883398`, branch `minimax/mcode-model-catalog`, worktree `~/apps/botfleet-mm-mcode`).**  Owner ask: run MiniMax-M3.1-Flash-Preview inside mcode.  Two real defects, both found by driving the installed 0.5.5 CLI rather than reading code.  First: mcode advertises its no-variant selection as `m:minimax:MiniMax-M3:v:` — a bare `v:` with an EMPTY variant — while `parseMcodeModelValue` accepted only the `u` suffix, so every no-variant value decoded to null, nothing matched, and plain `MiniMax-M3` was unselectable on any login.  Second: the catalog listed `MiniMax-M3` and `MiniMax-M2.7-highspeed`, neither of which a session advertises, and a row the session does not advertise fails the turn.  The shipped catalog is now the advertised set read off a live session and folded through `mcodePickerId`, with a test asserting every shipped row resolves against it.  `resolveModels` (the core's existing hook, used the same way by dsh) reads `defaultModel` and `defaultModelVariant` from the CLI's own `config.yaml` and adopts that as the default; it deliberately appends no rows, because a pre-session id cannot be verified.  Upstream quirk documented rather than papered over: 0.5.5 advertises the flash preview's no-variant value and then fails it on a real turn with an opaque `Internal error` (2 of 2 attempts), while its `v:thinking` sibling answers, so the catalog offers the working row.  Live proof: discovery reads a real `config.yaml`, returns `MiniMax-M3.1-Flash-Preview-thinking` as the default, a driver boot adopts it before any session, and a real turn answers `FLASH-OK`.  Gate: typecheck clean, 207 tests passing, all CI checks green.  Found and deliberately NOT fixed here: `~/.local/bin/mcode` is a symlink to a launcher that is not symlink-safe (it resolves its data dir from the parent of `$0`, so through the symlink it looks for `~/.local/current` and exits non-zero); a GUI-launched BotFleet inherits a minimal PATH, so which candidate `resolveCli` lands on decides whether the engine launches at all.  That belongs in a follow-up with its own verification.  - **2026-09-27 — MINIMAX — COMPLETED (PR #682 merged `44a10bc86`) — [BF][MINIMAX] Wire mcode as a fully supported CLI Engine (`mcodeAgent`) end to end (board `bf5513c80`, branch `minimax/mcode-cli-engine`, worktree `~/apps/botfleet-mm-mcode`).**  Owner ask: connect mcode as a fully supported CLI Engine in BotFleet.  PR #679 landed the ACP driver (`server/drivers/acp/mcode.ts`) and registered it in `BUILT_IN_DRIVERS`, but nothing downstream knew the engine existed — no capability row, no fleet instance, no quota mapping, no provider mark, no plan presets, no iOS mark, no docs row.  This change finishes the product surface.  mcode is a DISTINCT engine (id `mcode`, driver kind `mcodeAgent`, display name "MiniMax Code") that shares the MiniMax Token Plan and brand mark with the direct `minimax` engine, so it gets its own matrix row and badge one shade off MiniMax violet rather than being folded into an alias.  Server: `DEFAULT_FLEET` + `PRODUCT_FLEET_ADDITIONS` gain `mcode` so the engine exists on a fresh install and in an existing product config; `RESERVED_INSTANCE_ID` in `server/harness/registry.ts` gains `["mcodeAgent","mcode"]` so the default-fleet row is not misread as operator-added and offered a delete button; `driverKindsForWindow` now returns `mcodeAgent` for a MiniMax window and `quotaProviderForDriver("mcodeAgent")` answers `minimax`, which is what makes a mcode instance cappable and able to show a quota bar at all (deliberately NOT given an `ENGINE_METER_NOTES` row — that vocabulary is for engines with no window, and claiming one would contradict the window it just gained); `PROVIDER_BY_ENGINE` and `GEN_AI_PROVIDERS` map the driver kind onto `minimax` so Usage Monitor and Sentry bucket the spend correctly.  Renderer: full `mcode` entry in `src/lib/engine-capabilities.tsx` (files, terminal, this computer, web, image attachments, connected apps, cross-bot coordination, long context — each earned from the driver declaring `images: true` and the ACP core mounting MCP servers, which is why `connectedApps` is `yes` here and `no` on the direct MiniMax engine), plus `ENGINE_DISPLAY_ORDER`, `ENGINE_PLAN_OPTIONS`, `ProviderMark` (renders the MiniMax mark), and the two callout driver-kind lists.  iOS gets the MiniMax mark plus its own `MiniMax Code` VoiceOver label; the providers doc gets a built-in engines row.  NOT wired on purpose: `AUTO_FALLBACK_PRIORITY` is left alone because the ladder is instance-id keyed and mcode spends the same Token Plan as `minimax` — a second rung would double-count one wallet.  Usage attribution rides the engine-metadata path (`bucketEngine` → `engineIdFromDriverKind("mcodeAgent")` → `mcode`), so the shared `includes("minimax")` model-name heuristic in the legacy-bucket path was deliberately left untouched rather than risk re-attributing existing MiniMax history.  `MiniMax-M3` is listed under both engines, so `uniqueModelToEngineId()` correctly drops it as ambiguous instead of crediting one engine.  No context figure is claimed for the M2.7 highspeed tier because the CLI does not publish one.  LANDED #682 at 4:24pm Central, 21 files / +169.  Gate: pnpm typecheck clean; full test floor 5917 passed / 19 skipped across 405 files (5936 counted vs a floor of 1070); sub-suites 258 passed / 0 failed; all 12 CI checks green including Swift + iOS build and Seer review.  Live roundtrip closed PR #679's open gap — mcode 0.5.5 is installed on this Mac, the snapshot reported available and authenticated, the catalog returned MiniMax-M3 and MiniMax-M2.7-highspeed, and a real turn completed and answered as asked.  A lesson covering the full engine-wiring surface was contributed to fleet recall.

Rendered mentions visibly bolder and slightly larger (`font-bold text-[1.05em] text-accent`) across user message bubbles, plain text prompts, and bot markdown responses via a remark AST plugin (`remarkMentions`).  Handled multi-word app mentions (e.g. `#Google Sheets`) as a unified entity, and updated trigger detection to independently search for valid word-start triggers so embedded punctuation does not dismiss active queries.  22/22 unit tests green, full test suite passed, landed in PR #492, and deployed to Mac server via `ubf`. - **2026-09-18 — CLAUDE — PR #475 MERGED (`cd17a84d`), FOLLOW-UP IN PR #478 — Computer-capability parity: the verdict, one derivation, and the display drift behind it (board `7a3d30af`, branch `claude/computer-defaults-followup`, worktree `~/apps/botfleet-claude-computer-defaults`).**  VERDICT on the owner's parity ask: full parity is NOT reachable, and two of the four walls are not engineering-time problems.  No MCP *client* exists anywhere in this repo — every mount today is consumed by an external CLI that is its own client (`drivers/claude.ts:636`); `grok`, `minimax` and `openai-compat` read only `turn.toolHost` and never `turn.integrations`, so VPS and Local VM for them means writing the repo's first in-process MCP stdio client.  GUI control additionally needs vision and the HTTP lane is text-only (`chat-completions/loop.ts:148` types content as `string`; `TurnToolOutcome` has no image field) while `computer-proxy.ts:11` returns the frame in the same tool result as an MCP image block.  `openai-compat` targets arbitrary user endpoints, so uniform parity there is impossible by construction.  `boxAgent` runs on the ascii.dev substrate (`boxagent.ts:107` POSTs exactly `{provider, model, prompt}`) and cannot reach This Mac, a VPS or a Local VM from this repo at all.  Honest ceiling: 16/17 engines on Box — which needs NO MCP client, because `computer-proxy.ts` is already BotFleet's own minimal MCP server whose `call()` dispatcher is all REST and can be extracted as in-process executors — 16/17 on VPS/Local VM only if that client lane is funded, and `boxAgent` Box-only forever.  Steps for the HTTP-lane expansion belong on the pre-existing PLANNED row `da75e2da`, not here.  DO NOT set `computerMcp: true` on those drivers as an interim: it flips `mountsCloudComputer`, provisions a real billed box and claims a Local VM lease, for drivers whose `sendTurn` never reads the mount — a silent no-op that spends money.  #475 shipped what IS buildable: `server/computer-capability.ts` as one derived source of truth replacing three hand-copied server expressions plus a drifted client mirror, pinned by a test that re-derives the old expressions verbatim and asserts equality for all 17 engines; the client had ORed `computerMcp` into host-control support against the server's `localComputerMcp`-alone rule; `instanceSupportsCloudComputer` structurally could not see the cloud backend, so a Computer-engine bot on VPS was offered Cloud then died at `vps-computer.ts:1013`; and the consolidation's own fail-open lookup was held behind `engineReachKnown` so the `/api/instances` hydration window cannot fire a provision that starts a container or a billed box.  Survived a five-dimension adversarial review (7 findings, 5 survived, 0 blocking — the lead P1 was proven pre-existing on `main`).  Merged through main's PR #460 refactor, which had moved the rewired block into `resolveTurnComputerMounts`.  #478 finishes the owner's review nits: `ConfigStatusFrame` dropped `botDefaults` on every config SSE frame (`store.tsx:511` Pick, replace-reducer at `:1158`), so wiring the panels to the resolver would have silently reverted after the first broadcast — and the operator route that SETS the workspace default was itself one of the emitters that wiped it.  Plus one resolved `cloudBackend` const in ComputerPanel instead of two, the picker showing resolved while still writing raw, and `SettingsPanel:732` which hid the "Start VPS automatically" toggle from inheriting bots while `index.ts:10533` required it — a dead end.  Two user-visible bugs fixed in passing: the shared sleep handler showed a sleeping box instead of a stopped container, and the viewer-close POST never fired on the box branch, leaking an SSH desktop tunnel on the owner's own Mac.  Config-frame blocker and the ComputerPanel inventory came from a peer session that stood down rather than open a competing PR.  OPEN QUESTION for the owner: pinning `cloudBackend` is one-way — `index.ts:7909` accepts only box/vps and nothing clears it, so showing the resolved value makes an irreversible pin look like a no-op click.  Extra-ship no.

Merged origin/main through #468.  Extra-ship no.
- **2026-09-18 — CLAUDE — IN PR #474 — Carry the whole AgentBar quota handoff and let a spent subscription divert auto-fallback (branch `claude/quota-intake`, worktree `~/apps/botfleet-claude-quota-intake`).**  BotFleet kept 14 of the 22 per-window fields AgentBar publishes, hardcoded `skip: false`, and stored the rows where routing never saw them, so a spent subscription never diverted auto-fallback.  Now every field lands, plus the producer's newer `producer` and `issues` keys — an allow-listed provider that failed to read renders "Claude: <reason> (from AgentBar)" as plain text capped at 160 characters instead of vanishing from the grid.  `MODEL_TYPE_FAMILIES` maps AgentBar's own family names (`opus`, `sonnet`, `haiku`, `gemini`, `third-party`) onto catalog models, so a family-level window caps every model of that family instead of nothing; `driverKindsForWindow`, `modelsToSkip` and `isPlanLevelSkip` were added to, never reshaped.  One `NEAR_CAP_PERCENT` (exactly `<= 20`, 0 exhausted) now backs the engine chip, the grid cell and the derived status.  `applyLocalPayload` caps only on AgentBar's own `fileSkip`/`isExhausted` under `usage.localQuotaRouting` (default ON, Settings toggle); MiniMax, Antigravity and Grok Bot stay excluded.  Also repaired two raw control bytes commit `fef9fde8` wrote into `server/local-usage-monitor.ts`, which made the file binary to git.  Adversarial review (three lenses, three refuters each, 63 agents) then confirmed seven findings reducing to four defects, all fixed on this branch.  Every dictionary indexed by handoff text is read through `lookupOwn`, so a `modelType` or `window` of "constructor" can no longer resolve to `Object` and throw `function is not iterable` out of a fire-and-forget poll, or record a cooldown whose end is NaN.  The whole of `poll()` now runs under one `finally { this.inFlight = false; }` with the local half fenced in a try/catch of its own, so a bad handoff can neither strand the poller for eight days nor terminate the harness on every boot.  A `modelId` caps only when the instance catalog lists it, falling back to the row's family otherwise, so nothing is stored under a cooldown key no bot looks up. P3 `current-run.json`'s `logPath`/`progressPath` are accepted only inside the runs directory, because the status route publishes that log's tail to every client and to the paired phone. - **2026-09-16 — CLAUDE — COMPLETED (PR #450 merged) — Push hardening follow-up: eight confirmed post-merge review findings from #383 (branch `claude/push-hardening-followup`, lane `~/apps/botfleet-claude-push-followup`).**  P1 the sidecar's `drain()` shifted the next alert off its lane before asking whether a signing key was usable, so a key that faulted during the send immediately before silently swallowed whatever was queued behind it — unsent, never requeued and uncounted, on the very lane that exists so a blocking approval is never lost; the key is read first now. The harness frame's `seq` now rides in the push payload and a new `PushDeliveryLedger` in CompanionCore records what APNs has already shown, so the replay is recognised rather than drawn; a frame with no sequence is never suppressed, because silence is the worse failure.  P2 a thrown `fetch` got no retry at all while a 503 got three, so one reset connection lost an approval outright — transport failures now back off inside the attempt budget and report `0 SendFailed` only once it is spent.  P2 the fire-and-forget `void drain(...)` was unguarded, so a `forgetToken` that throws (it persists the device registry to disk) became an unhandled rejection and, under Node's default, an exit of the proxy every paired phone depends on.  P2 the watcher adopted the hello cursor even on a resumed stream, where that cursor is the tip and the gap is replayed after it, so a link that died before a replayed frame jumped the sidecar past everything it missed, permanently; it now makes the same cold-versus-resumed distinction the iOS client already makes.  P2 a failed lock-screen answer raised the app-wide "Something went wrong" modal on next foreground and cleared whatever was in `actionError` — `answerPendingRequest` answers `quietly` now, and the follow-up notification was already the user-facing report.  P3 the superseded-key guard compared object identity, so a .p8 rewritten with identical bytes read as a rotation and a genuine `InvalidProviderToken` for the key still in use was waved through while the health page kept saying pushes were on; it compares key identity via the digest the provider-token cache already computes.  P3 a key fault stranded whatever was already queued for a phone until an unrelated notification happened to arrive for that same phone; a reload that yields a usable key now sweeps the queues and restarts any that is idle and non-empty.  Seven new vitest cases, each mutation-checked against the unfixed code (48 in `companion/src/apns.test.ts`, 369 across `pnpm vitest run companion`); six new CompanionCore cases, 341 with `swift test --package-path ios`; both typechecks clean. Process detection moved from `ps` arguments to the kernel's open reference, because arguments are captured at exec and do not follow a rename — so the survivor check added in #371 could never fire — and the check now covers the whole bundle rather than the main binary alone, catching the embedded cua-driver and the speech and recorder helpers that the rename would otherwise displace underneath.  Also: orphan bundles are detected in the cache placement, an explicitly named stage is discovered and prune refuses when the generation just written is not among those found, legacy receipts that name no application are claimable by nobody rather than by whichever install happens to be called BotFleet, abandoned dependency candidates beside the checkout are swept, and a receipt stranded at `installing` is settled when it names the build that was installed and verified.

Two adversarial review rounds landed on top of the first cut: round one fixed a page that could splice the rotated generation over a gap, a blank panel when the newest record was wider than the tail window, a bot delete that orphaned every non-active task's logs (pre-existing on `main`), an unreclaimed temp file after a killed trim, and an untested bounded-read claim; round two fixed the fallback blocking the rotated top-up, a possible 64 MB `JSON.parse` on the Inspector's refresh path (now a stand-in row naming the record's size), a torn last line counted in the footer, a thread-id snapshot taken before two awaits, and transcript logs left behind by both task deletes and by a task merge (all pre-existing on `main`), plus a pid check that could throw and a boot line that led with the wrong clause. - **2026-09-13 — CLAUDE — COMPLETED (PR #371 merged) — Mac updater keeps rollback bundles out of /Applications, prunes older generations, and refuses to reopen over a survivor (branch `claude/updater-rollback-placement`, worktree `~/apps/botfleet-claude-updater-rollback`).**  The prior bundle now moves into the update's own private stage at `<stage>/rollback/BotFleet.app` instead of a hidden `.BotFleet.rollback-….app` beside the installed app, because a dot-bundle in `/Applications` is still indexed by Spotlight and Launchpad and, since macOS binds a running process to the bundle it launched from, any process that outlives the swap shows the rollback name in the Dock until it is relaunched — which is what the owner saw after the Sep 12 install. Quiescence now also requires that no process runs from the installed bundle, and the transaction refuses to reopen the app while any process survives in the rollback bundle, since `open` activates an existing instance of the bundle identifier rather than launching the replacement.

- **2026-09-13 — CLAUDE — COMPLETED (PR #383 merged) — Push hardening: time-sensitive kinds, retries, cached provider token, sender health, lock-screen Approve/Deny (branch `claude/push-hardening`, worktree `~/apps/botfleet-claude-push-hardening`).**  The harness notify `kind` never reached the phone: the sidecar parsed a notify frame, dropped `notification.kind`, and sent every push as an undifferentiated alert with no `interruption-level`, no `relevance-score` and no category — so an approval a bot was blocked on arrived exactly as loudly as a routine that finished, and no lock-screen action could be attached to one.  `kind` now travels end to end (approval/question land time-sensitive in `BOTFLEET_APPROVAL`, the rest `active` in `BOTFLEET_UPDATE`, approvals ranked highest), the sender retries a 429 for the `Retry-After` Apple asked for and a 5xx with bounded exponential backoff while 410 still clears the token and 400/403 log once per device with Apple's `reason`, the provider JWT is signed once per twenty minutes instead of once per push and re-signed immediately on `ExpiredProviderToken`, a missing `.p8` is re-checked every five minutes instead of disabling the watcher forever, and the sender's health is exposed on the control `/state` and a new authenticated `GET /api/companion/push-health` — counts, timestamps and Apple's own status strings only.  On the phone, `BOTFLEET_APPROVAL` and `BOTFLEET_UPDATE` are finally registered as `UNNotificationCategory` objects with Approve, Deny and Open actions routed to the same answer path `AnswerApprovalIntent` uses, so an approval can be settled from the lock screen with the app closed.

- **2026-09-13 — CLAUDE — COMPLETED (PR #386 merged) — Chat-completions lane: bounded abort-aware retry with backoff for transient HTTP and network failures (branch `claude/mm-chat-completions-retry`, worktree `~/apps/botfleet-claude-mm-retry`).**  There was no retry or backoff anywhere under `server/drivers/chat-completions/`, so a single 502 or a reset socket failed a MiniMax turn outright where Claude and Codex ride the same hiccup out silently through `server/drivers/retry.ts` — found by the botfleet-02 CLAUDE session's provider-parity audit (G5), severity P1.  `loop.ts` now wraps each round's request in a bounded attempt loop, so every driver on the loop inherits it rather than MiniMax alone: at most three attempts, backoff from the fleet's one existing schedule in `drivers/retry.ts` (1s then 3s, ±25% jitter) instead of a second private policy, and the classification split so `errors.ts` says what a status is worth and `loop.ts` decides what to do about it.  Retried: 408, 500/502/503/504, and network failures through the same `classifyError` text classifier every CLI driver already uses; a 429 gets the full schedule when it named a `Retry-After` (honoured, capped at 30s) and exactly one polite retry when it did not, so a real quota wall still reaches the fallback ladder as `quota_or_region_restriction` instead of disappearing into a backoff nobody's balance outlasts; 400/401/403/404/413/422 and the permanent 5xx (501, 505) are never retried, and classification after the last failed attempt is byte-for-byte what it was.  Two rules keep it honest: a round that already PUBLISHED anything — a text delta, a reasoning delta, a tool call announced off the stream, reported by the driver through the new `onPublished` seam on `runRound` — is never retried, because replaying it would show a person text they have already read; and an attempt that reported usage is settled and billed rather than silently redone, which is what makes "a failed attempt's usage is never summed" true by construction instead of by throwing real tokens away.  An attempt also never starts without 20s of headroom on BOTH the round's own 180s ceiling — shared across every attempt at that round, not re-armed per try, so two retries cannot quietly turn 180s into nine minutes — and the turn's 900s wall clock, and the backoff sleep is abort-aware on the round's combined signal, so Stop, a sweep or the round deadline during a wait settles at once.  The single-terminal-event invariant does not move: the attempt loop sits INSIDE one round, every exit still falls through the same one `finally`, the closed `TurnLoopExit` union is unchanged, and a retried turn is still exactly one `turn.completed`.  Each retry emits one `turn.retrying` — the same event every CLI driver already emits — so the routine receipt stays quiet and keeps the run running, the Sentry span takes a warning breadcrumb, and `index.ts` renders its standard "retrying — attempt N/3" activity chip; `turn.completed` has no metadata bag to carry a `retries` count, so the count stays where every other engine already reports it.  `minimax.ts` changed only at the request function's seam (`res.headers` into `httpErrorFor`, `onPublished` from the two stream callbacks), and `openai-compat.ts`, `grok.ts`, `tool-executor.ts`, `turn-tools.ts` and the `isHttpDriver` / `isToolCallsStopReason` sites were left alone for CODEX's #355.  Three review findings on the PR were real and fixed in 0d88cc82: a parsed `Retry-After` is now propagated on EVERY retryable branch (a 503 or a 408 that names a cool-down was having it dropped and falling back to the ~1s schedule, retrying into the same outage); a 429 that reaches the loop as bare text — a driver still throwing `new Error("HTTP 429")` — gets the same one polite retry the parsed one does, through a shared `UNHINTED_RATE_LIMIT_ATTEMPTS`, so the policy cannot depend on which driver threw it; and `turn.retrying` gained an optional `maxAttempts` so the activity chip names THIS failure's ceiling instead of always printing `RETRY_MAX_ATTEMPTS` and reading "attempt 2/3" right before a no-cool-down 429 gave up after attempt 2.  Verified: `pnpm typecheck` clean; 361 targeted tests green across `chat-completions/**`, `minimax.test.ts`, `http-lane-e2e.test.ts`, `thread-events.test.ts` and `index.test.ts` with 34 rows new (20 policy rows in `loop.test.ts`, 13 in `errors.test.ts`, 2 end-to-end in `minimax.test.ts` against the real `fake-openai-server.ts` socket, plus the existing 502 row extended to prove a persistent outage still spends exactly three requests and settles once); the whole local vitest suite green at 3906 passed / 19 skipped over 300 files, and every `pnpm test` script after it green except one row in `test:release-workflow` that cannot resolve `electron-builder` because this lane's `node_modules` had to be cloned from a lockfile-identical sibling (the registry was unreachable from the sandbox) — CI settles it, with `typecheck + test` green on ubuntu, macOS and Windows; `oxlint` reports no finding on the touched source files that the branch did not already carry on `main`.  Vercel is red repo-wide on a deployment rate limit, unrelated.  Review round: Codex's five threads settled on the branch — `Retry-After` now propagates on every retryable status and `turn.retrying` carries THIS failure's own `maxAttempts` so the chip never promises an attempt that is not coming (both in `678a1f23`), HTTP 529 joins the retryable server set so the plan's own simulated-529 criterion actually holds, Node's `TypeError: terminated` / `UND_ERR_SOCKET` premature close is recognised by shape in `errors.ts`'s `isPrematureCloseError` so a 200 SSE that dies before its first delta is retried instead of read as terminal, and the bare-text 429 seam already spends the same one polite retry a parsed no-`Retry-After` 429 gets so no future driver on the loop can earn a more generous policy by throwing a plain `Error`.

- **2026-09-27 — MINIMAX — IN PR — [BF][MINIMAX] Owner-facing DSH model list with 2x / Preview / Multimodal chips (board `f7778a2a`, branch `minimax/dsh-model-list`, worktree `~/apps/botfleet-minimax-dsh-models`, PR #688).**  Owner: the DSH picker should offer DeepSeek V4 Pro, DeepSeek V4 Flash, MiniMax M3.1 Flash Preview, M3, and M2.7 Highspeed, with M2.7 Highspeed flagged as 2x the money.  `STATIC_DSH_MODELS` was inherited from the Harness package and only ever subtracted from, so it could not express any of this; it is now stated directly and the now-unused `harnessDshModels` import is gone.  Three chips carry facts rather than decoration.  Multimodal on deepseek-v4-flash: DeepSeek's catalog is only TWO models — `deepseek-flash` IS the image/video model, and its image tokens are billed together with text at the same rate, so there is deliberately no third DeepSeek row and the chip states a capability, not a price.  Preview on MiniMax-M3.1-Flash-Preview: MiniMax's own OpenAI SDK docs say it is available only through Token Plan and MiniMax Code, so it needs a Token Plan key and the chip says so instead of letting a turn fail on a pay-as-you-go key.  2x Cost on MiniMax-M2.7-highspeed: $0.60/$2.40 against M3's $0.30/$1.20 at the same 204,800 context.  Every chip carries a badgeTitle, because a bare chip is not a price, and a test enforces ModelCatalog.badge's ~10 char ceiling — the first draft used 'Image + Video' at 13 chars and the test forced the rename to 'Multimodal' with the specifics moved to the hover.  Seer raised a HIGH on the PR warning the new MiniMax ids were untested and that a hardcoded allowlist in `dshProviderForModel` would make them dead choices.  Checked before agreeing: it is prefix based, so both ids encode to ["minimax", <id>] and decode unchanged — the risk did not materialise, but the coverage gap was real.  Two tests added: one loops the whole catalog so a future model is covered the day it lands, and one spells out the two newer ids so a regression from prefix matching to a list fails with a readable message instead of at turn time.  49/49 in dsh.test.ts, typecheck clean, `lint` now green repo-wide.  Extra-ship no.

- **2026-09-27 - MINIMAX - COMPLETED (PR #685 merged `093472050`) - [BF][MINIMAX] Bot settings: two blue toggles named Connected Apps, neither names Composio.** <!-- wb-agent-report:4e79d69149c8464e911b3b460ca1afbd -->  Owner ask from a screenshot: these options are confusing and should say they relate to Composio.  Diagnosis found two problems, not one: (1) the second card's LIT toggle meant LESS access — it sat as a peer under the grant card with both lit, and "limited" is the one state a switch position cannot convey, since every other switch in that panel means on = the bot may do the thing; (2) neither card said the Gmail/Calendar/Slack accounts belong to Composio, so a grant that hands a bot your mail read as a BotFleet built-in.  Card 1 is now "Composio Connected Apps" and names Composio in all four states;  its not-set-up branch pointed at "App Settings" generically and now names App Settings → Connections, which is where the service is configured (and `configured` is the SERVICE, not the account list, so "no account connected yet" would have been a false claim).  Card 2 is now "Restrict To Specific Apps" and states both toggle directions in BOTH states, and it is now a visual sub-setting (indented, recessed, left rail) whose textarea went back to the raised surface so the field keeps its affordance.  The four-way blurb moved out of the JSX ternary into `src/lib/connected-apps-copy.ts` so the copy is testable.  No behavior change.  18 tests green in the two touched suites, `pnpm typecheck` clean, all 12 hosted checks success (lint PR gate, gitleaks, Seer, Swift+iOS, typecheck+test on macOS/Ubuntu/Windows), zero lint violations added.  Full-repo `pnpm lint` fails on `origin/main` before this branch (4,043 vs a 4,019 baseline — FINCH's merge-commit drift in #666), so no baseline update was needed or made.
- **2026-09-27 - MINIMAX - IN_PROGRESS - [BF][MINIMAX] Wire mcode as a fully supported CLI Engine (mcodeAgent) end to end.** <!-- wb-agent-report:bf5513c800064bab8d647aefd085a293 -->
- **2026-09-27 — MINIMAX — IN PR — [BF][MINIMAX] Live model discovery for every engine: shared discovery module, live xAI and DSH catalogs (board `64b987e3`, branch `minimax/model-auto-discovery`, worktree `~/apps/botfleet-minimax-model-discovery`).**  Owner ask: make BotFleet automatically get updated model listings for all engines.  The audit found the real gap was not that every engine was hardcoded, but that no shared mechanism existed, so each driver hand-rolled its own TTL and three engines never got one.  New `server/drivers/model-discovery.ts` owns the shape every API-key engine shares: `fetchProviderModels` (`GET {baseUrl}/models`, normalized across the bare-array and `{data:[]}` shapes, never throws), `mergeDiscoveredModels` (keeps the hand-written label, badge, and contextWindow for ids already known, takes the provider's own `name` for a new one, drops an excluded id, and returns `null` rather than an empty picker), and `createModelDiscoveryProbe` / `discoverModelCatalog` (one memoized probe so concurrent describes share an in-flight request instead of fanning out — MiniMax memoed at 60 s, Cursor at 5 min, opencode kept a last-good, three engines memoed nothing).  `grok` (xAI API) now discovers live from `GET {url}/models` at a 60 s TTL, and returns `models` through a getter so the reassignment actually reaches the picker.  `dshAgent` now reads the Harness install's own `~/.dsh/settings.yaml` (`llm-pi-ai.providers.<id>.models[]`) instead of trusting the catalog frozen into the Harness package, plugging into the `resolveModels` hook `acp/core.ts` already calls so the existing boot-deadline race and last-usable-catalog fallback apply unchanged.  The live read UNIONS rather than replaces, and that is load-bearing: a DSH profile is a partial source (this Mac's declares only the `minimax` block while the static catalog is deepseek-v4-flash / deepseek-v4-pro / MiniMax-M3), so a wholesale replace would have silently deleted both DeepSeek rows and the static default.  `readClaudeModelCatalog` already unions the same way, so this follows the house pattern;  a partial source can add models but never retire them, and retirement stays an explicit `DSH_EXCLUDED_MODEL_IDS` entry.  On the live install the read adds `MiniMax-M2.7-highspeed` and drops nothing, asserted by a test against the real settings file.  `MiniMax-M2.7` exclusion expressed once in `DSH_EXCLUDED_MODEL_IDS` so the live read and the static fallback cannot drift.  Deliberately static still: `boxAgent` (the ascii.dev box service exposes no models endpoint to discover from) and `qwenAgent` (local injects only, by design).  Rollout doc with the full 16-engine source audit: `docs/rollouts/2026-09-27-live-model-discovery.md`.  52 new tests (27 discovery, 9 grok, 16 dsh) plus `pnpm typecheck` clean and the full `pnpm test` gate green.  Three real bugs surfaced mid-work and are now guarded by tests: (1) `readDshSettingsPath` used `homedir()` instead of `environment.HOME`, reading the live settings file and ignoring a relocated home;  (2) `createModelDiscoveryProbe` cleared its memo in a `finally`, so every settled probe forgot itself and the TTL bought nothing for sequential callers;  (3) the DSH reader looked for a top-level `providers` map when the real file nests it at `llm-pi-ai.providers`, so the first version was dead code on this machine.  Extra-ship no.  <!-- wb-agent-report:64b987e317374b0fba22ab981f0cd294 -->

- **2026-09-09 — CLAUDE — IN PR #357 — MM PR 10/11: typed HTTP errors, honest MiniMax snapshot, MiniMax on the failover ladder (board `83e9a706`, branch `claude/mm-10-typed-errors-snapshot-ladder`, worktree `~/apps/botfleet-claude-mm-10`, parent `cdba649d`).**  New `server/drivers/chat-completions/errors.ts` maps an HTTP status onto the shared `ProviderErrorCode` union the ACP drivers already speak — 401/403 to `invalid_credentials` with `setup: true`, 402/429 to `quota_or_region_restriction`, 404 to `model_catalog_outage`, 5xx to `upstream_outage` — while a status the family has no opinion on (400, 422) stays a plain `Error` and keeps today's behaviour byte for byte.  `chat-completions/loop.ts` turns a classified `ProviderError` into an `error:<code>` terminal stopReason and puts `setup: true` on the `runtime.error` chip, so a revoked key reaches the setup affordance instead of idling behind a red chip; the single-terminal invariant is untouched, still exactly one `turn.completed` per turn on every exit.  `model-fallback.ts` gains `providerErrorCodeFromStopReason` and `quotaOrCapFromErrorCode`, and `index.ts` prefers the structured code over regexing chip prose when one is present — both strictly additive, returning `undefined` with no structured code so a CLI engine is decided by the existing regexes unchanged; a 502 now consults the fallback chain where it previously just failed, and `invalid_credentials` deliberately does not, because another engine does not fix a bad key.  The #90 auto-failover ladder moves out of `index.ts` into the exported `AUTO_FALLBACK_PRIORITY`, unit-testable without booting the server, with `minimax` after `codex` and ahead of `openaiCompat` per the owner-approved default, handed straight to `eligibleAutoFallbackChain` so main's health-and-cooldown filtering is unchanged.  `minimax.ts`'s `snapshot()` stops reading Available-until-a-turn-fails: a GET /models probe with an 8s ceiling, cached 60s and shared with `refreshModels` as one fetch with two readers, reads unavailable-with-a-reason on 401/403, available-but-capped on 402/429, and keeps the last confirmed state on a 5xx, a 404, or a network failure rather than flipping a working key on a blip; `refreshModels` reads the live catalog off that same fetch, retiring the hand-maintained three-model table to a fallback, so a new MiniMax model no longer needs a code change and a release.  `pnpm typecheck` clean; 156 targeted tests green across errors/loop/model-fallback/minimax; oxlint clean on the two new files.  Local gate limited to typecheck plus the touched test files, full suite on hosted CI, per the Sep 12 fleet load pause. <!-- wb-agent-report:83e9a70679b4410da29a1b00089d47af -->

- **2026-09-04 - CLAUDE - COMPLETED - VPS desktop budget: size for a shared host, make it configurable.**  Landed in BotFleet #197.  The per-desktop cap was one constant shared by the Local VM and the VPS path.  That is right for the Local VM (sole desktop on the person's own workstation, 8 GiB / 4 CPUs) and wrong for a VPS, which runs one desktop PER BOT on one machine — the same number fits two bots on a 30 GB server.  VPS desktops now default to 3 GiB / 2 CPUs and are configurable via vps.memoryGib / vps.cpus, bounded 2-64 GiB and 1-32 CPUs.  dockerSecurityIsHardened takes the caps to expect rather than reading the Local VM constants; still an exact comparison, because a container built to a size the caller did not ask for is one the caller no longer controls.  Two test fixtures had the old numbers hardcoded in fake docker inspect responses — one surfaced as a 30s timeout reading 'the echoed turn never happened' — and now derive from the same defaults the run args use. <!-- wb-agent-report:75d53f85482643b0be069ef7f5e5c74d -->
- **2026-09-04 - CLAUDE - COMPLETED - Off button granted the bot the user's Mac; [] and unset were the same state.**  Fixed in BotFleet #200.  bot.computers had three meanings collapsed into two representations: undefined (never configured, auto), [] (Off, or deselecting the last destination), and [...] (those destinations).  The server read it as 'bot.computers ?? []', so [] became auto, auto reached the host fallback, and on macOS shouldMountLocalComputer({requested: undefined}) returns true — an explicitly-Off bot got the user's own Mac.  ComputerPanel already treated [] as off, so the UI and the server disagreed.  Extracted the rule into resolveGrants() with tests, and split ComputerDestination (cloud|vm|local, what you pick) from ComputerKind (box|vps|vm|local, what mounts) since 'cloud' has no mount kind of its own. <!-- wb-agent-report:32aa4deecb32467ba7960840642eccfe -->

## 2026-09-24 — MINIMAX — Bound replay-history byte cap across minimax/grok/openai-compat drivers (Codex review 09-24)

**Branch:** `minimax/botfleet-codex-review` @ `164759e4` (worktree `~/apps/botfleet-minimax`).  **PR:** [#591](https://github.com/jaywedgeworth22/BotFleet/pull/591).

**Why:** Codex review (thread `01a08556-967c-7043-b6e9-dc207265ad7d`, board `92effec6`, audit doc TBD) flagged that the three API-backed transcript-replay drivers — `minimax.ts`, `grok.ts`, `openai-compat.ts` — fold the FULL thread transcript into every chat-completions request.  Because the loop re-sends a byte-identical prefix on every round to keep the provider's prompt cache reachable, an oversized transcript gets re-uploaded on every tool call too.  Long threads grow past the model's prompt window or the per-request size cap, and a single pasted stack trace multiplies the cost across rounds.  This is the largest replay-driven token cost in the driver layer.

**What:** New shared helper `server/drivers/chat-completions/replay-cap.ts` exporting `capReplayedTranscript(transcript, { maxBytes?, maxEntries? })`.  Walks the transcript from newest to oldest, trims the OLDEST entries until the kept prefix fits inside both caps (defaults: 200 KiB / 60 entries), and preserves the most recent tool-call / tool-result pair even when the byte cut would orphan one half — preferring an oversized prefix over a malformed chat-completions message that 400s the request.  Counts UTF-8 bytes (not JS chars) so multibyte text is budgeted at the same rate the provider's tokenizer will see it.  Wired into the three drivers' `sendTurn` before they build the messages array.  System prompt and current user turn are always kept (uncapped); only the prior transcript is trimmed.

**Tests:** 11 unit tests for the helper + 3 driver-level regression tests (one per driver, asserting that an 80×5 KiB transcript is trimmed before it reaches the request body).  `pnpm typecheck` clean; 105 driver tests green (replay-cap 11 + grok 15 + minimax 49 + openai-compat 30).  Existing short-transcript tests preserved — the cap only trims over-budget transcripts.

**Files:**
- `server/drivers/chat-completions/replay-cap.ts` — new helper
- `server/drivers/chat-completions/replay-cap.test.ts` — new unit tests
- `server/drivers/minimax.ts`, `server/drivers/grok.ts`, `server/drivers/openai-compat.ts` — wire cap into sendTurn
- `server/drivers/{minimax,grok,openai-compat}.test.ts` — driver-level regression tests

**Followups:** None blocking.  Cap is configurable per-driver if a future model wants a larger budget (the helper accepts overrides); no other chat-completions driver in this repo uses the transcript-replay pattern (claude/codex/antigravity/etc. are CLI drivers with their own session state).  The 97-finding audit's other findings (telemetry collector resends, DSH gap, Grok 4.7 catalog staleness) remain open under their existing board items.

## 2026-09-24 — MINIMAX — Claude audit 09-24 DR7 + RH4 (retry.ts regex tighten + .tmp-pr-body hygiene)

**Branch:** `minimax/botfleet-claude-fixes-20260924` @ `ba1448b2` (worktree `~/apps/botfleet-minimax`).  **PR:** [#595](https://github.com/jaywedgeworth22/BotFleet/pull/595).

**Why:** The 2026-09-24 efficiency audit (Claude, 97 findings, `docs/audits/2026-09-24-efficiency-audit.md`) flagged DR7 (P2) and RH4 (P3) as items Claude's 17-lane fix program does not cover.  DR7 is `retry.ts:37-60` -- the classifier's loose regex set was matching benign substrings ("billing address", "subscribe to our newsletter", "page 402 of the changelog", "section 503 of the spec") and classifying successful turns as permanent give-up instead of letting the driver retry.  RH4 is the tracked `.tmp-pr-body.md` scratch file + the throwaway scripts a seat drops at the repo root while debugging (debug-cap.mjs, test-auto*, test-verdict*).

**What:**
1. `server/drivers/retry.ts` -- extract `hasHttpStatusCode()` + `classifyByStatusCode()`, tighten loose patterns.  Numeric codes now anchor to a status-code cue (HTTP, status:, code:, error:, or start-of-input).  Quota-noun terms now require an error verb (exceeded, reached, disabled, expired, etc.) or be one of the named phrases the existing tests rely on.
2. `server/drivers/retry.test.ts` -- 4 new "does not fire" tests + 1 "still recognizes" test.
3. `.gitignore` -- added `.tmp-pr-body*`, `.tmp-pr-*.md`, `debug-cap.mjs`, `debug-*.{cjs,mjs,js}`, `test-auto*`, `test-verdict*` to the existing scratch-file block.
4. `.tmp-pr-body.md` deleted.

**Tests:** 14 retry tests pass (10 existing + 4 new).  Existing tests preserved -- no input previously classified as quota/auth/rate_limit lands somewhere else now.  `pnpm typecheck` clean.

**Files:**
- `server/drivers/retry.ts` -- regex tightening + new helpers
- `server/drivers/retry.test.ts` -- 4 new regression tests
- `.gitignore` -- scratch-file patterns
- `.tmp-pr-body.md` (deleted)

**Refs:** `docs/audits/2026-09-24-efficiency-audit.md` (DR7, RH4).  MiniMax focus on items Claude's 17-lane fix program does not cover.

## 2026-09-24 — MINIMAX — Fleet-wide Vercel deploy guard (2h cap per owner directive)

Owner directive 2026-09-24: "vercel should only update for any site if there are updates to the site and no more than once every 2 hours unless manually deployed".  The fleet had a 1h-cap guard (`vercel-ignore-hourly.sh`) at four sites that was already plumbed through `vercel.json`'s `ignoreCommand`.  BotFleet, ContactLogo, and Personal-Site had the script on disk but never `git add`ed, so Vercel's checkout didn't actually have the guard.  Fleet-OPS and Autorotate had no guard at all.

**Changes:**

- `vercel-ignore-hourly.sh` → cap `3600` (1 hour) → `7200` (2 hours); comments updated to match.  Live in 4 sites now tracked (DealDex, BotFleet, ContactLogo, Personal-Site).  Time-cap update, no logic change.
- New `vercel-ignore.sh` (2h cap) added to Fleet-OPS and Autorotate with repo-appropriate watch paths (`apps/web/` nested in Autorotate; repo-root minus `docs`, `datadog`, AGENT-*, PLAN, PROJECT, STATUS, `*.md` excludes in Fleet-OPS).  `vercel.json` `ignoreCommand` wired to point at it.
- Manual override unchanged: `VERCEL_FORCE_DEPLOY=1` (env) or Dashboard Redeploy with Ignore Build Step unchecked.

### 2026-09-25 — CLAUDE — Sentry Seer autofix land stage (bf-seer-triage): #625 idle-guard follow-up, #626/#627 closed

Land stage for the four unclaimed Sentry Seer autofix PRs triaged earlier this session (#604 closed already, #607→#626, #608→#625, #609→#627).  Adversarial review verdicts: #625 `fix_required`, #626 `close`, #627 `close`.

- **#625 (merged already, before this stage's review completed):** it shipped the P0 fix (unattended per-request ceiling 180s → 900s) but with none of the reviewer's P2/P3 findings applied.  Real gap: nothing distinguished a slow-but-live stream from a fully hung connection, so a stall now held the bot for the full 900s instead of the old 180s — board row `bf77b434` (Sentry BOTFLEET-V) already tracks that exact failure shape.  Follow-up PR [#629](https://github.com/jaywedgeworth22/BotFleet/pull/629) (`claude/sentry-minimax-idle-timeout`) adds `readChunkOrStall`: races each SSE `reader.read()` against a 120s idle timer, scoped to the unattended path only (`idleTimeoutMs` set only when `turn.unattended`).  A stall now fails as a retryable `provider_error` in ~120s (up to 3 attempts, ~380s total) instead of riding the full 900s; a merely-slow stream (any single gap under 120s) is unaffected.  Also fixed the two P3 findings (stale doc-citing comment; documented why the override is MiniMax-only).  Added the 3 tests #625 shipped without (slow-but-live survives past 180s, attended ceiling still fires at 180s, stalled stream fails via the new guard) — all fake-timer-based.  `tsc -b` + `tsc -p tsconfig.server.json` clean; `vitest run server/drivers` 902 passed/1 skipped/0 failed; oxlint zero NEW findings vs `origin/main` baseline.  Auto-merge armed.
- **#626 (fix(ios) code-block hang, closed, not landed):** review measured the UITextView/TextKit2 replacement as 2–30x SLOWER than the SwiftUI `Text` it replaced (Mac Catalyst probe, 3000-line mixed-script block: 4.9–9.0s vs 0.18–0.89s) — a non-scrolling `UITextView` still lays out its full intrinsic size synchronously on the main thread, so TextKit 2's off-viewport-layout advantage never applies here.  Closed with the full measured findings; BOTFLEET-Y/Z remain open.
- **#627 (perf(ios) lazy-load, closed, not landed):** review reproduced two real regressions from the `fleet(messages: 1)` hydrate stub — pending approval cards that aren't a thread's newest message drop off the Approvals screen, and a `resumed:false` reconnect leaves a permanent transcript gap with no repair path.  It also doesn't fix the hang it targets (`ChatView`'s eager layout reloads the same 50-message cost once a chat opens).  Closed with the full findings; BOTFLEET-Y/Z remain open.
- Filed board `9615bf46` (P1, unclaimed) for the still-open iOS code-block hang (BOTFLEET-Y/Z) with the review's recommended direction (bounded/chunked rendering, e.g. per-line `Text` rows in a `LazyVStack`, or a rendered-line cap) since closing #626/#627 leaves it with no active PR.
- Cleaned up the three now-dead `~/apps/botfleet-claude-sentry-{607-codeblock-hang,608-minimax-timeout,609-lazy-load}` worktrees.
